```
    16→// TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_FromTranscript tests that when Claude commits
    17→// mid-session (before Stop has been called), the prepare-commit-msg hook detects
    18→// the new work by checking the live transcript and adds a checkpoint trailer.
    19→//
    20→// This is scenario 2 from ENT-112:
    21→// - User prompts Claude
    22→// - Claude creates files and commits them
    23→// - No Stop has happened yet (no shadow branch)
    24→// - The commit should still get a checkpoint trailer because the transcript shows file modifications
    25→func TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_FromTranscript(t *testing.T) {
    26→	t.Parallel()
    27→
    28→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
    29→
    30→	session := env.NewSession()
    31→
    32→	// Simulate user prompt (initializes session with BaseCommit and TranscriptPath)
    33→	// We need to pass the transcript path so it gets stored in session state
    34→	input := map[string]string{
    35→		"session_id":      session.ID,
    36→		"transcript_path": session.TranscriptPath,
    37→	}
    38→	inputJSON, _ := json.Marshal(input)
    39→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", "user-prompt-submit")
    40→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
    41→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
    42→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
    43→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+env.ClaudeProjectDir,
    44→	)
    45→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
    46→		t.Fatalf("user-prompt-submit failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
    47→	}
    48→
    49→	// Verify session state has transcript path
    50→	state, err := env.GetSessionState(session.ID)
    51→	if err != nil {
    52→		t.Fatalf("Failed to get session state: %v", err)
    53→	}
    54→	if state == nil {
    55→		t.Fatal("Session state is nil")
    56→	}
    57→	if state.TranscriptPath == "" {
    58→		t.Error("Session state should have TranscriptPath after user-prompt-submit")
    59→	}
    60→	t.Logf("Session state TranscriptPath: %s", state.TranscriptPath)
    61→
    62→	// Create a file as if Claude wrote it
    63→	env.WriteFile("claude_file.txt", "content from Claude")
    64→
    65→	// Create transcript showing Claude created the file (NO Stop called)
    66→	session.CreateTranscript("Create a file for me", []FileChange{
    67→		{Path: "claude_file.txt", Content: "content from Claude"},
    68→	})
    69→
    70→	// Verify NO shadow branch exists (Stop hasn't been called)
    71→	shadowBranches := env.ListBranchesWithPrefix("entire/")
    72→	hasShadowBranch := false
    73→	for _, b := range shadowBranches {
    74→		if b != paths.MetadataBranchName {
    75→			hasShadowBranch = true
    76→			break
    77→		}
    78→	}
    79→	if hasShadowBranch {
    80→		t.Error("Shadow branch should not exist before Stop is called")
    81→	}
    82→
    83→	// Get HEAD before commit
    84→	headBefore := env.GetHeadHash()
    85→
    86→	// Commit with shadow hooks - should add trailer because transcript shows file modifications
    87→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Add file from Claude (mid-session)", "claude_file.txt")
    88→
    89→	// Get the commit
    90→	commitHash := env.GetHeadHash()
    91→	if commitHash == headBefore {
    92→		t.Fatal("Commit was not created")
    93→	}
    94→
    95→	// CRITICAL: Verify commit has a checkpoint trailer
    96→	// This is the fix for ENT-112 scenario 2: detect work from live transcript
    97→	checkpointID := env.GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitHash)
    98→	if checkpointID == "" {
    99→		t.Error("Mid-session commit should have Entire-Checkpoint trailer when transcript shows file modifications")
   100→	} else {
   101→		t.Logf("Mid-session commit has checkpoint ID: %s", checkpointID)
   102→	}
   103→}
   104→
   105→// TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_NoTrailerWithoutTranscriptPath tests that
   106→// when TranscriptPath is not set in session state, commits don't get erroneous
   107→// checkpoint trailers (graceful fallback).
   108→func TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_NoTrailerWithoutTranscriptPath(t *testing.T) {
   109→	t.Parallel()
   110→
   111→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
   112→
   113→	session := env.NewSession()
   114→
   115→	// Simulate user prompt WITHOUT transcript path (legacy behavior)
   116→	if err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID); err != nil {
   117→		t.Fatalf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v", err)
   118→	}
   119→
   120→	// Create a file manually (not through Claude)
   121→	env.WriteFile("manual_file.txt", "manual content")
   122→
   123→	// Don't create transcript - simulating a case where transcript path isn't available
   124→
   125→	// Commit with shadow hooks
   126→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Manual commit without transcript", "manual_file.txt")
   127→
   128→	// Commit should NOT have checkpoint trailer (no session activity detected)
   129→	commitHash := env.GetHeadHash()
   130→	checkpointID := env.GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitHash)
   131→	if checkpointID != "" {
   132→		t.Errorf("Commit without session activity should not have checkpoint trailer, got: %s", checkpointID)
   133→	}
   134→}
   135→
   136→// TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_NoTrailerForUnrelatedFile tests that
   137→// when Claude has modified files but the committed file is unrelated,
   138→// no checkpoint trailer is added.
   139→func TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_NoTrailerForUnrelatedFile(t *testing.T) {
   140→	t.Parallel()
   141→
   142→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
   143→
   144→	session := env.NewSession()
   145→
   146→	// Simulate user prompt with transcript path
   147→	input := map[string]string{
   148→		"session_id":      session.ID,
   149→		"transcript_path": session.TranscriptPath,
   150→	}
   151→	inputJSON, _ := json.Marshal(input)
   152→	cmd := exec.Command(getTestBinary(), "hooks", "claude-code", "user-prompt-submit")
   153→	cmd.Dir = env.RepoDir
   154→	cmd.Stdin = bytes.NewReader(inputJSON)
   155→	cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(),
   156→		"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="+env.ClaudeProjectDir,
   157→	)
   158→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
   159→		t.Fatalf("user-prompt-submit failed: %v\nOutput: %s", err, output)
   160→	}
   161→
   162→	// Create transcript showing Claude modified a DIFFERENT file
   163→	session.CreateTranscript("Create a file", []FileChange{
   164→		{Path: "claude_file.txt", Content: "content from Claude"},
   165→	})
   166→
   167→	// Create and commit an UNRELATED file (not in transcript)
   168→	env.WriteFile("unrelated_file.txt", "unrelated content")
   169→
   170→	// Commit with shadow hooks - should NOT add trailer because files don't overlap
   171→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooks("Unrelated file commit", "unrelated_file.txt")
   172→
   173→	commitHash := env.GetHeadHash()
   174→	checkpointID := env.GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitHash)
   175→
   176→	// CRITICAL: No checkpoint trailer should be added for unrelated files
   177→	// The overlap check in sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript ensures this
   178→	if checkpointID != "" {
   179→		t.Errorf("Unrelated file commit should NOT have checkpoint trailer, but got: %s", checkpointID)
   180→	} else {
   181→		t.Log("Correctly omitted checkpoint trailer for unrelated file commit")
   182→	}
   183→}
   184→
   185→// TestShadowStrategy_AgentCommit_AlwaysGetsTrailer tests that when an agent commits
   186→// (ACTIVE session + no TTY), the trailer is always added regardless of content
   187→// detection. This is the fast path that bypasses transcript analysis.
   188→func TestShadowStrategy_AgentCommit_AlwaysGetsTrailer(t *testing.T) {
   189→	t.Parallel()
   190→
   191→	env := NewFeatureBranchEnv(t, strategy.StrategyNameManualCommit)
   192→
   193→	session := env.NewSession()
   194→
   195→	// Start session (sets phase to ACTIVE)
   196→	if err := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID); err != nil {
   197→		t.Fatalf("SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v", err)
   198→	}
   199→
   200→	// Create a file and commit as agent (no TTY)
   201→	env.WriteFile("agent_file.txt", "created by agent")
   202→	env.GitCommitWithShadowHooksAsAgent("Agent commit", "agent_file.txt")
   203→
   204→	// Agent commits should ALWAYS get a trailer (fast path, no content detection)
   205→	commitHash := env.GetHeadHash()
   206→	checkpointID := env.GetCheckpointIDFromCommitMessage(commitHash)
   207→	if checkpointID == "" {
   208→		t.Error("Agent commit during ACTIVE session should always get a checkpoint trailer")
   209→	} else {
   210→		t.Logf("Agent commit correctly got checkpoint trailer: %s", checkpointID)
   211→	}
   212→}
```

```
  1400→type CheckpointValidation struct {
  1401→	// CheckpointID is the expected checkpoint ID

  1404→	// SessionID is the expected session ID

  1406→
  1407→	// Strategy is the expected strategy name

  1416→	// ExpectedTranscriptContent are strings that should appear in full.jsonl
  1417→	ExpectedTranscriptContent []string
  1418→
  1419→	// CheckpointsCount is the expected checkpoint count (0 means don't validate)
  1420→	CheckpointsCount int
  1421→}
  1422→
  1423→// ValidateCheckpoint performs comprehensive validation of a checkpoint on the metadata branch.
  1424→// It validates:
  1425→// - Root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary) structure and expected fields
  1426→// - Session metadata.json (CommittedMetadata) structure and expected fields
  1427→// - Transcript file (full.jsonl) is valid JSONL and contains expected content
  1428→// - Content hash file (content_hash.txt) matches SHA256 of transcript
  1429→// - Prompt file (prompt.txt) contains expected prompts
  1430→func (env *TestEnv) ValidateCheckpoint(v CheckpointValidation) {
  1431→	env.T.Helper()
  1432→
  1433→	// Validate root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary)
  1434→	env.validateCheckpointSummary(v)
  1435→
  1436→	// Validate session metadata.json (CommittedMetadata)
  1437→	env.validateSessionMetadata(v)
  1438→
  1439→	// Validate transcript is valid JSONL
  1440→	env.validateTranscriptJSONL(v.CheckpointID, v.ExpectedTranscriptContent)
  1441→
  1442→	// Validate content hash matches transcript
  1443→	env.validateContentHash(v.CheckpointID)
  1444→
  1445→	// Validate prompt.txt contains expected prompts
  1446→	if len(v.ExpectedPrompts) > 0 {
  1447→		env.validatePromptContent(v.CheckpointID, v.ExpectedPrompts)
  1448→	}
  1449→}
  1450→
  1451→// validateCheckpointSummary validates the root metadata.json (CheckpointSummary).
  1452→func (env *TestEnv) validateCheckpointSummary(v CheckpointValidation) {
  1453→	env.T.Helper()
  1454→
  1455→	summaryPath := CheckpointSummaryPath(v.CheckpointID)
  1456→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, summaryPath)
  1457→	if !found {
  1458→		env.T.Fatalf("CheckpointSummary not found at %s", summaryPath)
  1459→	}
  1460→
  1461→	var summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary
  1462→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary); err != nil {
  1463→		env.T.Fatalf("Failed to parse CheckpointSummary: %v\nContent: %s", err, content)
  1464→	}
  1465→
  1466→	// Validate checkpoint_id
  1467→	if summary.CheckpointID.String() != v.CheckpointID {
  1468→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.CheckpointID = %q, want %q", summary.CheckpointID, v.CheckpointID)
  1469→	}
  1470→
  1471→	// Validate strategy
  1472→	if v.Strategy != "" && summary.Strategy != v.Strategy {
  1473→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.Strategy = %q, want %q", summary.Strategy, v.Strategy)
  1474→	}
  1475→
  1476→	// Validate sessions array is populated
  1477→	if len(summary.Sessions) == 0 {
  1478→		env.T.Error("CheckpointSummary.Sessions should have at least one entry")
  1479→	}
  1480→
  1481→	// Validate files_touched
  1482→	if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
  1483→		touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
  1484→		for _, f := range summary.FilesTouched {
  1485→			touchedSet[f] = true
  1486→		}
  1487→		for _, expected := range v.FilesTouched {
  1488→			if !touchedSet[expected] {
  1489→				env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.FilesTouched missing %q, got %v", expected, summary.FilesTouched)
  1490→			}
  1491→		}
  1492→	}
  1493→
  1494→	// Validate checkpoints_count
  1495→	if v.CheckpointsCount > 0 && summary.CheckpointsCount != v.CheckpointsCount {
  1496→		env.T.Errorf("CheckpointSummary.CheckpointsCount = %d, want %d", summary.CheckpointsCount, v.CheckpointsCount)
  1497→	}
  1498→}
  1499→
  1500→// validateSessionMetadata validates the session-level metadata.json (CommittedMetadata).
  1501→func (env *TestEnv) validateSessionMetadata(v CheckpointValidation) {
  1502→	env.T.Helper()
  1503→
  1504→	metadataPath := SessionMetadataPath(v.CheckpointID)
  1505→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, metadataPath)
  1506→	if !found {
  1507→		env.T.Fatalf("Session metadata not found at %s", metadataPath)
  1508→	}
  1509→
  1510→	var metadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
  1511→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &metadata); err != nil {
  1512→		env.T.Fatalf("Failed to parse CommittedMetadata: %v\nContent: %s", err, content)
  1513→	}
  1514→
  1515→	// Validate checkpoint_id
  1516→	if metadata.CheckpointID.String() != v.CheckpointID {
  1517→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.CheckpointID = %q, want %q", metadata.CheckpointID, v.CheckpointID)
  1518→	}
  1519→
  1520→	// Validate session_id
  1521→	if v.SessionID != "" && metadata.SessionID != v.SessionID {
  1522→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.SessionID = %q, want %q", metadata.SessionID, v.SessionID)
  1523→	}
  1524→
  1525→	// Validate strategy
  1526→	if v.Strategy != "" && metadata.Strategy != v.Strategy {
  1527→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.Strategy = %q, want %q", metadata.Strategy, v.Strategy)
  1528→	}
  1529→
  1530→	// Validate created_at is not zero
  1531→	if metadata.CreatedAt.IsZero() {
  1532→		env.T.Error("CommittedMetadata.CreatedAt should not be zero")
  1533→	}
  1534→
  1535→	// Validate files_touched
  1536→	if len(v.FilesTouched) > 0 {
  1537→		touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
  1538→		for _, f := range metadata.FilesTouched {
  1539→			touchedSet[f] = true
  1540→		}
  1541→		for _, expected := range v.FilesTouched {
  1542→			if !touchedSet[expected] {
  1543→				env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.FilesTouched missing %q, got %v", expected, metadata.FilesTouched)
  1544→			}
  1545→		}
  1546→	}
  1547→
  1548→	// Validate checkpoints_count
  1549→	if v.CheckpointsCount > 0 && metadata.CheckpointsCount != v.CheckpointsCount {
  1550→		env.T.Errorf("CommittedMetadata.CheckpointsCount = %d, want %d", metadata.CheckpointsCount, v.CheckpointsCount)
  1551→	}
  1552→}
  1553→
  1554→// validateTranscriptJSONL validates that full.jsonl exists and is valid JSONL.
  1555→func (env *TestEnv) validateTranscriptJSONL(checkpointID string, expectedContent []string) {
  1556→	env.T.Helper()
  1557→
  1558→	transcriptPath := SessionFilePath(checkpointID, paths.TranscriptFileName)
  1559→	content, found := env.ReadFileFromBranch(paths.MetadataBranchName, transcriptPath)
  1560→	if !found {
  1561→		env.T.Fatalf("Transcript not found at %s", transcriptPath)
  1562→	}
  1563→
  1564→	// Validate it's valid JSONL (each non-empty line should be valid JSON)
  1565→	lines := strings.Split(content, "\n")
  1566→	validLines := 0
  1567→	for i, line := range lines {
  1568→		line = strings.TrimSpace(line)
  1569→		if line == "" {
  1570→			continue
  1571→		}
  1572→		validLines++
  1573→		var obj map[string]any
  1574→		if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(line), &obj); err != nil {
  1575→			env.T.Errorf("Transcript line %d is not valid JSON: %v\nLine: %s", i+1, err, line)
  1576→		}
  1577→	}
  1578→
  1579→	if validLines == 0 {
  1580→		env.T.Error("Transcript is empty (no valid JSONL lines)")
  1581→	}
  1582→
  1583→	// Validate expected content appears in transcript
  1584→	for _, expected := range expectedContent {
  1585→		if !strings.Contains(content, expected) {
  1586→			env.T.Errorf("Transcript should contain %q", expected)
  1587→		}
  1588→	}
  1589→}
  1590→

  1598→	if !found {
```

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's a summary of what was added:

**Summary of changes:**

1. **Fixed fallback logic** in `manual_commit_condensation.go` (previous work) - Added `hadFilesBeforeFiltering` flag to distinguish:
   - Session had files but none overlapped → NO fallback (unrelated session)
   - Session had no files to begin with → fallback to `committedFiles` (mid-session commit)

2. **Added unit tests** in `manual_commit_test.go` (previous work):
   - `TestCondenseSession_FilesTouchedFallback_EmptyState` - fallback works when `FilesTouched` was empty
   - `TestCondenseSession_FilesTouchedNoFallback_NoOverlap` - no fallback when `FilesTouched` had files but none overlapped

3. **Added integration test** in `mid_session_commit_test.go` (just now):
   - `TestShadowStrategy_MidSessionCommit_FilesTouchedFallback` - end-to-end test verifying that when a mid-session commit happens with empty `FilesTouched` in session state, the checkpoint metadata correctly receives the committed files via the fallback

The fix prevents the bug where 7 sessions all had `files_touched = ["testenv.go"]` even though only one of them actually touched that file - the incorrect fallback was assigning `committedFiles` to sessions that had files in their `FilesTouched` but none of them overlapped with the commit.

```
    14→// Content-aware overlap detection for checkpoint management.
    15→//
    16→// These functions determine whether a commit contains session-related work by comparing
    17→// file content (not just filenames) against the shadow branch. This enables accurate
    18→// detection of the "reverted and replaced" scenario where a user:
    19→// 1. Reverts session changes (e.g., git checkout -- file.txt)
    20→// 2. Creates completely different content in the same file
    21→// 3. Commits the new content
    22→//
    23→// In this scenario, the commit should NOT get a checkpoint trailer because the
    24→// session's work was discarded, not incorporated.
    25→//
    26→// The key distinction:
    27→// - Modified files (exist in parent commit): Always count as overlap, regardless of
    28→//   content changes. The user is editing session's work.
    29→// - New files (don't exist in parent): Require content match against shadow branch.
    30→//   If content differs completely, the session's work was likely reverted & replaced.
    31→
    32→// filesOverlapWithContent checks if any file in filesTouched overlaps with the committed
    33→// content, using content-aware comparison to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
    34→//
    35→// This is used in PostCommit to determine if a session has work in the commit.
    36→func filesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowBranchName string, headCommit *object.Commit, filesTouched []string) bool {
    37→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
    38→
    39→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
    40→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
    41→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
    42→		touchedSet[f] = true
    43→	}
    44→
    45→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
    46→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
    47→	if err != nil {
    48→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
    49→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    50→		)
    51→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    52→	}
    53→
    54→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's content)
    55→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
    56→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: shadow branch not found, falling back to filename check",
    59→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
    60→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    61→		)
    62→		return len(filesTouched) > 0 // Fall back: assume overlap if any files touched
    63→	}
    64→
    65→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
    66→	if err != nil {
    67→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to filename check",
    68→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    69→		)
    70→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    71→	}
    72→
    73→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
    74→	if err != nil {
    75→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to filename check",
    76→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
    77→		)
    78→		return len(filesTouched) > 0
    79→	}
    80→
    81→	// Get the parent commit tree to determine if files are modified vs newly created.
    82→	// For modified files (exist in parent), we count as overlap regardless of content
    83→	// because the user is editing the session's work.
    84→	// For newly created files (don't exist in parent), we check content to detect
    85→	// the "reverted and replaced" scenario where user deleted session's work and
    86→	// created something completely different.
    87→	var parentTree *object.Tree
    88→	if headCommit.NumParents() > 0 {
    89→		if parent, err := headCommit.Parent(0); err == nil {
    90→			if pTree, err := parent.Tree(); err == nil {
    91→				parentTree = pTree
    92→			}
    93→		}
    94→	}
    95→
    96→	// Check each file in filesTouched
    97→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
    98→		// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)
    99→		headFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)
   100→		if err != nil {
   101→			// File not in HEAD commit. This happens when:
   102→			// - The session created/modified the file but user deleted it before committing
   103→			// - The file was staged as a deletion (git rm)
   104→			// In both cases, the session's work on this file is not in the commit,
   105→			// so it doesn't contribute to overlap. Continue checking other files.
   106→			continue
   107→		}
   108→
   109→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file
   110→		isModified := false
   111→		if parentTree != nil {
   112→			if _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {
   113→				isModified = true
   114→			}
   115→		}
   116→
   117→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   118→		if isModified {
   119→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   120→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   121→			)
   122→			return true
   123→		}
   124→
   125→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   126→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   127→		if err != nil {
   128→			// File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
   129→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
   130→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   131→			)
   132→			continue
   133→		}
   134→
   135→		// Compare by hash (blob hash) - exact content match required for new files
   136→		if headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {
   137→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   138→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   139→				slog.String("hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   140→			)
   141→			return true
   142→		}
   143→
   144→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   145→			slog.String("file", filePath),
   146→			slog.String("head_hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   147→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   148→		)
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   152→		slog.Int("files_checked", len(filesTouched)),
   153→	)
   154→	return false
   155→}

   157→// stagedFilesOverlapWithContent checks if any staged file overlaps with filesTouched,
   158→// distinguishing between modified files (always overlap) and new files (check content).
   159→//
   160→// For modified files (already exist in HEAD), we count as overlap because the user
   161→// is editing the session's work. For new files (don't exist in HEAD), we require
   162→// content match to detect the "reverted and replaced" scenario.
   163→//
   164→// This is used in PrepareCommitMsg for carry-forward scenarios.
   165→func stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo *git.Repository, shadowTree *object.Tree, stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   166→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   167→
   168→	// Build set of filesTouched for quick lookup
   169→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   170→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   171→		touchedSet[f] = true
   172→	}
   173→
   174→	// Get HEAD tree to determine if files are being modified or newly created
   175→	head, err := repo.Head()
   176→	if err != nil {
   177→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD, falling back to filename check",
   178→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   179→		)
   180→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   181→	}
   182→	headCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   183→	if err != nil {
   184→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD commit, falling back to filename check",
   185→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   186→		)
   187→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   188→	}
   189→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   190→	if err != nil {
   191→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get HEAD tree, falling back to filename check",
   192→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   193→		)
   194→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   195→	}
   196→
   197→	// Get the git index to access staged file hashes
   198→	idx, err := repo.Storer.Index()
   199→	if err != nil {
   200→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get index, falling back to filename check",
   201→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   202→		)
   203→		return hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched)
   204→	}
   205→
   206→	// Build a map of index entries for O(1) lookup (avoid O(n*m) nested loop)
   207→	indexEntries := make(map[string]plumbing.Hash, len(idx.Entries))
   208→	for _, entry := range idx.Entries {
   209→		indexEntries[entry.Name] = entry.Hash
   210→	}
   211→
   212→	// Check each staged file
   213→	for _, stagedPath := range stagedFiles {
   214→		if !touchedSet[stagedPath] {
   215→			continue // Not in filesTouched, skip
   216→		}
   217→
   218→		// Check if this is a modified file (exists in HEAD) or new file
   219→		_, headErr := headTree.File(stagedPath)
   220→		isModified := headErr == nil
   221→
   222→		// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)
   223→		if isModified {
   224→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap",
   225→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   226→			)
   227→			return true
   228→		}
   229→
   230→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   231→		stagedHash, found := indexEntries[stagedPath]
   232→		if !found {
   233→			continue // Not in index (shouldn't happen but be safe)
   234→		}
   235→
   236→		// Get file from shadow branch tree
   237→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(stagedPath)
   238→		if err != nil {
   239→			// File not in shadow branch - can't verify content overlap.
   240→			// Don't assume overlap just because it's in filesTouched.
   241→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: file not in shadow tree, skipping",
   242→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   243→			)
   244→			continue
   245→		}
   246→
   247→		// Compare hashes - exact match means file is unchanged
   248→		if stagedHash == shadowFile.Hash {
   249→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   250→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   251→				slog.String("hash", stagedHash.String()),
   252→			)
   253→			return true
   254→		}
   255→
   256→		// Hashes differ - check if there's significant content overlap.
   257→		// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from
   258→		// "reverted and replaced" (user wrote completely different content).
   259→		shadowContent, shadowErr := shadowFile.Contents()
   260→		if shadowErr != nil {
   261→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read shadow content",
   262→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   263→				slog.String("error", shadowErr.Error()),
   264→			)
   265→			continue
   266→		}
   267→
   268→		// Read staged content from object store
   269→		stagedBlob, blobErr := repo.BlobObject(stagedHash)
   270→		if blobErr != nil {
   271→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read staged blob",
   272→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   273→				slog.String("error", blobErr.Error()),
   274→			)
   275→			continue
   276→		}
   277→		stagedReader, readerErr := stagedBlob.Reader()
   278→		if readerErr != nil {
   279→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to get staged reader",
   280→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   281→				slog.String("error", readerErr.Error()),
   282→			)
   283→			continue
   284→		}
   285→		stagedBytes, readErr := io.ReadAll(stagedReader)
   286→		_ = stagedReader.Close() // Best effort close
   287→		if readErr != nil {
   288→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: failed to read staged content",
   289→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   290→				slog.String("error", readErr.Error()),
   291→			)
   292→			continue
   293→		}
   294→		stagedContent := string(stagedBytes)
   295→
   296→		// Check for significant content overlap
   297→		if hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent) {
   298→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file has partial overlap (partial staging)",
   299→				slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   300→			)
   301→			return true
   302→		}
   303→
   304→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: new file has no significant overlap (reverted & replaced)",
   305→			slog.String("file", stagedPath),
   306→			slog.String("staged_hash", stagedHash.String()),
   307→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   308→		)
   309→	}
   310→
   311→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   312→		slog.Int("staged_files", len(stagedFiles)),
   313→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   314→	)
   315→	return false
   316→}
   317→
   318→// hasOverlappingFiles checks if any file in stagedFiles appears in filesTouched.
   319→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   320→func hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, filesTouched []string) bool {
   321→	touchedSet := make(map[string]bool)
   322→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   323→		touchedSet[f] = true
   324→	}
   325→
   326→	for _, staged := range stagedFiles {
   327→		if touchedSet[staged] {
   328→			return true
   329→		}
   330→	}
   331→	return false
   332→}
   333→
   334→// filesWithRemainingAgentChanges returns files from filesTouched that still have
   335→// uncommitted agent changes. This is used for carry-forward after partial commits.
   336→//
   337→// A file has remaining agent changes if:
   338→//   - It wasn't committed at all (not in committedFiles), OR
   339→//   - It was committed but the committed content doesn't match the shadow branch
   340→//     (user committed partial changes, e.g., via git add -p)
   341→//
   342→// Falls back to file-level subtraction if shadow branch is unavailable.
   343→func filesWithRemainingAgentChanges(
   344→	repo *git.Repository,
   345→	shadowBranchName string,
   346→	headCommit *object.Commit,
   347→	filesTouched []string,
   348→	committedFiles map[string]struct{},
   349→) []string {
   350→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   351→
   352→	// Get HEAD commit tree (the committed content)
   353→	commitTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   354→	if err != nil {
   355→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get commit tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   356→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   357→		)
   358→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   359→	}
   360→
   361→	// Get shadow branch tree (the session's full content)
   362→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   363→	shadowRef, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   364→	if err != nil {
   365→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: shadow branch not found, falling back to file subtraction",
   366→			slog.String("branch", shadowBranchName),
   367→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   368→		)
   369→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   370→	}
   371→
   372→	shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())
   373→	if err != nil {
   374→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow commit, falling back to file subtraction",
   375→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   376→		)
   377→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   378→	}
   379→
   380→	shadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()
   381→	if err != nil {
   382→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: failed to get shadow tree, falling back to file subtraction",
   383→			slog.String("error", err.Error()),
   384→		)
   385→		return subtractFilesByName(filesTouched, committedFiles)
   386→	}
   387→
   388→	var remaining []string
   389→
   390→	for _, filePath := range filesTouched {
   391→		// If file wasn't committed at all, it definitely has remaining changes
   392→		if _, wasCommitted := committedFiles[filePath]; !wasCommitted {
   393→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   394→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not committed, keeping",
   395→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   396→			)
   397→			continue
   398→		}
   399→
   400→		// File was committed - check if committed content matches shadow branch
   401→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   402→		if err != nil {
   403→			// File not in shadow branch - nothing to carry forward for this file
   404→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in shadow branch, skipping",
   405→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   406→			)
   407→			continue
   408→		}
   409→
   410→		commitFile, err := commitTree.File(filePath)
   411→		if err != nil {
   412→			// File not in commit tree (deleted?) - keep it if it's in shadow
   413→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   414→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: file not in commit tree but in shadow, keeping",
   415→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   416→			)
   417→			continue
   418→		}
   419→
   420→		// Compare hashes - if different, there are still uncommitted agent changes
   421→		if commitFile.Hash != shadowFile.Hash {
   422→			remaining = append(remaining, filePath)
   423→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content mismatch, keeping for carry-forward",
   424→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   425→				slog.String("commit_hash", commitFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   426→				slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()[:7]),
   427→			)
   428→		} else {
   429→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: content fully committed",
   430→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   431→			)
   432→		}
   433→	}
   434→
   435→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesWithRemainingAgentChanges: result",
   436→		slog.Int("files_touched", len(filesTouched)),
   437→		slog.Int("committed_files", len(committedFiles)),
   438→		slog.Int("remaining_files", len(remaining)),
   439→	)
   440→
   441→	return remaining
   442→}
   443→
   444→// subtractFilesByName returns files from filesTouched that are NOT in committedFiles.
   445→// This is a fallback when content-aware comparison isn't possible.
   446→func subtractFilesByName(filesTouched []string, committedFiles map[string]struct{}) []string {
   447→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   448→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "subtractFilesByName: ",
   449→		slog.Any("filesTouched", filesTouched),
   450→		slog.Any("committedFiles", committedFiles),
   451→	)
   452→	var remaining []string
   453→	for _, f := range filesTouched {
   454→		if _, committed := committedFiles[f]; !committed {
   455→			remaining = append(remaining, f)
   456→		}
   457→	}
   458→	return remaining
   459→}
   460→
   461→// hasSignificantContentOverlap checks if two file contents share significant lines.
   462→// This distinguishes partial staging (user kept some agent content) from
   463→// "reverted and replaced" (user wrote completely different content).
   464→//
   465→// For larger files, we require at least 2 matching lines because a single match
   466→// (like "package main") is likely common boilerplate. For small files (≤ 2
   467→// significant lines in either file), any single match counts as overlap since
   468→// there aren't many lines to match anyway.
   469→//
   470→// The function filters out trivial lines (short lines < 10 chars like braces,
   471→// empty lines, etc.) because these commonly appear in many files and don't
   472→// indicate meaningful overlap.
   473→func hasSignificantContentOverlap(stagedContent, shadowContent string) bool {
   474→	// Build set of significant lines from shadow (agent) content
   475→	shadowLines := extractSignificantLines(shadowContent)
   476→
   477→	// Build set of significant lines from staged (user) content
   478→	stagedLines := extractSignificantLines(stagedContent)
   479→
   480→	// If either has no significant lines, no meaningful overlap possible
   481→	if len(shadowLines) == 0 || len(stagedLines) == 0 {
   482→		return false
   483→	}
   484→
   485→	// For very small files (0-1 significant lines), any single match is meaningful
   486→	// since requiring 2 matches would be impossible. The 2-line requirement is
   487→	// mainly to filter out coincidental boilerplate (like "package main") in
   488→	// larger files where we CAN require multiple matches.
   489→	isVerySmallFile := len(shadowLines) < 2 || len(stagedLines) < 2
   490→	requiredMatches := 2
   491→	if isVerySmallFile {
   492→		requiredMatches = 1
   493→	}
   494→
   495→	// Count matching lines
   496→	matchCount := 0
   497→	for line := range stagedLines {
   498→		if shadowLines[line] {
   499→			matchCount++
   500→			if matchCount >= requiredMatches {
   501→				return true
   502→			}
   503→		}
   504→	}
   505→
   506→	return false
   507→}
   508→
   509→// extractSignificantLines returns a set of significant lines from content.
   510→// Lines are trimmed and must be at least 10 characters.
   511→//
   512→// This simple length-based filter works well because:
   513→// - Most structural boilerplate is short: `{`, `}`, `});`, `} else {` (all < 10 chars)
   514→// - Long lines (>= 10 chars) are usually meaningful regardless of language
   515→// - No language-specific pattern matching needed
   516→func extractSignificantLines(content string) map[string]bool {
   517→	lines := make(map[string]bool)
   518→	for _, line := range splitLines([]byte(content)) {
   519→		trimmed := trimLine(line)
   520→		if len(trimmed) >= 10 {
   521→			lines[trimmed] = true
   522→		}
   523→	}
   524→	return lines
   525→}
   526→
   527→// trimLine removes leading and trailing whitespace from a line.
   528→func trimLine(line string) string {
   529→	start := 0
   530→	end := len(line)
   531→	for start < end && (line[start] == ' ' || line[start] == '\t') {
   532→		start++
   533→	}
   534→	for end > start && (line[end-1] == ' ' || line[end-1] == '\t') {
   535→		end--
   536→	}
   537→	return line[start:end]
   538→}
   539→
```

```
   527→// shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck returns true if the session should be condensed
   528→// into this commit. Requires both that hasNew is true AND that the session's files
   529→// overlap with the committed files with matching content.
   530→//
   531→// This prevents stale sessions (ACTIVE sessions where the agent was killed, or
   532→// ENDED/IDLE sessions with carry-forward files) from being condensed into every
   533→// unrelated commit.
   534→//
   535→// filesTouchedBefore is populated from:
   536→//   - state.FilesTouched for IDLE/ENDED sessions (set via SaveStep/SaveTaskStep -> mergeFilesTouched)
   537→//   - transcript extraction for ACTIVE sessions when FilesTouched is empty
   538→//
   539→// When filesTouchedBefore is empty:
   540→//   - For ACTIVE sessions: fail-open (trust hasNew) because the agent may be
   541→//     mid-turn before any files are saved to state.
   542→//   - For IDLE/ENDED sessions: return false because there are no files to
   543→//     overlap with the commit.
   544→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck(isActive bool) bool {
   545→	if !h.hasNew {
   546→		return false
   547→	}
   548→	if len(h.filesTouchedBefore) == 0 {
   549→		return isActive // ACTIVE: fail-open; IDLE/ENDED: no files = no overlap
   550→	}
   551→	// Only check files that were actually changed in this commit.
   552→	// Without this, files that exist in the tree but weren't changed
   553→	// would pass the "modified file" check in filesOverlapWithContent
   554→	// (because the file exists in the parent tree), causing stale
   555→	// sessions to be incorrectly condensed.
   556→	var committedTouchedFiles []string
   557→	for _, f := range h.filesTouchedBefore {
   558→		if _, ok := h.committedFileSet[f]; ok {
   559→			committedTouchedFiles = append(committedTouchedFiles, f)
   560→		}
   561→	}
   562→	if len(committedTouchedFiles) == 0 {
   563→		return false
   564→	}
   565→	return filesOverlapWithContent(h.repo, h.shadowBranchName, h.commit, committedTouchedFiles)
   566→}
   567→
   568→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleDiscardIfNoFiles(state *session.State) error {
   569→	if len(state.FilesTouched) == 0 {
   570→		logging.Debug(h.logCtx, "post-commit: skipping empty ended session (no files to condense)",
   571→			slog.String("session_id", state.SessionID),
   572→		)
   573→	}
   574→	h.s.updateBaseCommitIfChanged(h.logCtx, state, h.newHead)
   575→	return nil
   576→}
   577→
   578→func (h *postCommitActionHandler) HandleWarnStaleSession(_ *session.State) error {
   579→	// Not produced by EventGitCommit; no-op for exhaustiveness.
   580→	return nil
   581→}
   582→
   583→// During rebase/cherry-pick/revert operations, phase transitions are skipped entirely.
   584→//
   585→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
   586→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PostCommit() error {
   587→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   588→
   589→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   590→	if err != nil {
   591→		return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   592→	}
   593→
   594→	// Get HEAD commit to check for trailer
   595→	head, err := repo.Head()
   596→	if err != nil {
```

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. Let me answer the user's question:

**The overlap check (`shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck`) is part of this PR (PR 418) - it didn't exist before.**

Looking at the git history:
- `744c4f3f` "fix ENDED sessions with carry-forward re-condensed into every commit" - **added `shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck`**
- `014f53f2` "fix IDLE sessions with empty FilesTouched incorrectly condensed" - extended the fix

Both commits are on the current branch (`alex/fix-multi-session-vacuums-too-much`), not on main.

The checkpoint `6e19340bc0c8` was created **before** this PR was merged. At that time:
1. Sessions were condensed based on `hasNew` alone
2. There was **no overlap check** between `FilesTouched` and committed files
3. The fallback bug assigned `testenv.go` to all 7 sessions' `files_touched` metadata
4. All 7 sessions got condensed into the commit without any verification

**To answer your specific question about the current code:** If a file is in `FilesTouched` but NOT in the shadow branch, `filesOverlapWithContent` handles it correctly:

```go
// content_overlap.go:126-132
shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
if err != nil {
    // File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
    logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
        slog.String("file", filePath),
    )
    continue  // Skip this file, don't count as overlap
}
```

If **all** files in `filesTouched` are not in the shadow branch, the function returns `false` and the session is NOT condensed. So there's no bug here - the current fix handles this correctly.

The summary:
- **Pre-PR-418**: No overlap check existed → sessions condensed incorrectly
- **Post-PR-418**: Overlap check verifies files exist in shadow branch with matching content → prevents false condensation

> AGENT

I found the bug. There's a mismatch between `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent` (used in prepare-commit-msg) and `filesOverlapWithContent` (used in post-commit) for deleted files:

**In `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent` (prepare-commit-msg):**
- Checks if file exists in HEAD BEFORE commit
- Deleted file exists in HEAD before deletion → `isModified = true` → returns overlap
- Trailer is added

**In `filesOverlapWithContent` (post-commit):**
- Checks HEAD tree AFTER commit (where file is gone)
- `headTree.File(filePath)` fails → file is skipped
- Returns `false` → no condensation

Result: Checkpoint trailer is added but metadata is never written (orphaned checkpoint ID).

Let me check how staged deletions are represented to understand the fix:

I see the issue now. The fix should check if a file is being deleted (exists in HEAD but not in index) before treating it as a modified file. Let me implement this fix:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_019JhzEgC9zRzR3KS93dVNEx
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/content_overlap.go",
  "old_string": "\t// Check each staged file\n\tfor _, stagedPath := range stagedFiles {\n\t\tif !touchedSet[stagedPath] {\n\t\t\tcontinue // Not in filesTouched, skip\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Check if this is a modified file (exists in HEAD) or new file\n\t\t_, headErr := headTree.File(stagedPath)\n\t\tisModified := headErr == nil\n\n\t\t// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)\n\t\tif isModified {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", stagedPath),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Check each staged file\n\tfor _, stagedPath := range stagedFiles {\n\t\tif !touchedSet[stagedPath] {\n\t\t\tcontinue // Not in filesTouched, skip\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Check if file is in the index (staged content)\n\t\tstagedHash, inIndex := indexEntries[stagedPath]\n\n\t\t// Check if this is a modified file (exists in HEAD) or new file\n\t\t_, headErr := headTree.File(stagedPath)\n\t\texistsInHead := headErr == nil\n\n\t\t// Handle deletions: file exists in HEAD but not in index (or zero hash)\n\t\t// Deletions don't count as overlap - there's no content to compare.\n\t\t// This matches the behavior in filesOverlapWithContent where deleted files\n\t\t// (not in the commit tree) are skipped.\n\t\tisDeleted := existsInHead && (!inIndex || stagedHash.IsZero())\n\t\tif isDeleted {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: deleted file skipped (no content to compare)\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", stagedPath),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Modified files (exists in HEAD and in index) always count as overlap\n\t\t// (user edited session's work)\n\t\tif existsInHead {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"stagedFilesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", stagedPath),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}"
}
```

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's a summary of the fix:

## Bug Analysis

The E2E test `TestE2E_DeletedFiles_CommitDeletion` was failing because there was a mismatch between two overlap-checking functions when handling **deleted files**:

**`stagedFilesOverlapWithContent`** (used in prepare-commit-msg):
- Checked if file exists in HEAD (before commit)
- For deleted files: file exists in HEAD before deletion → `isModified = true` → returned `true`
- Added checkpoint trailer

**`filesOverlapWithContent`** (used in post-commit):
- Checked if file exists in HEAD tree (after commit)
- For deleted files: file is gone from commit tree → skipped → returned `false`
- Did NOT condense the session

Result: Orphaned checkpoint ID with no metadata written.

## Fix

Modified `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent` to detect deletions (file exists in HEAD but not in index or has zero hash) and skip them, matching the behavior of `filesOverlapWithContent`:

```go
// Handle deletions: file exists in HEAD but not in index (or zero hash)
// Deletions don't count as overlap - there's no content to compare.
isDeleted := existsInHead && (!inIndex || stagedHash.IsZero())
if isDeleted {
    continue
}
```

## Added Test

Added `TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_DeletedFile` to verify deleted files don't count as overlap.

> DEVELOPER

does this make sense in the context of the other bugs?

> AGENT

Good question. Let me trace through how this fits with the other bugs in this PR.

**The theme of PR 418** is preventing sessions from being incorrectly linked to commits they don't belong to ("vacuums too much").

**The bugs fixed so far:**

1. **`shouldCondenseWithOverlapCheck`** - Added overlap checking so sessions only condense if `filesTouchedBefore ∩ committedFileSet` has matching content

2. **`FilesTouched` fallback bug** - Sessions with non-overlapping files incorrectly got `committedFiles` assigned to their metadata

3. **This fix (deleted files)** - Mismatch between prepare-commit-msg and post-commit for deletions

**But here's the question:** Does it even make sense for a deletion commit to get a checkpoint trailer?

Consider the scenario:
1. Agent deletes `to_delete.go` via `rm` (Bash command - NOT tracked in `FilesTouched`)
2. Agent creates `replacement.go` via Write tool (tracked)
3. User commits `replacement.go` → checkpoint created, transcript condensed
4. User commits deletion → what value does a checkpoint add here?

The deletion commit has **no content to restore in a rewind**. The session transcript was already captured in step 3. Linking this commit to the session would just create a duplicate entry.

So the fix is consistent with the PR's theme:
- Don't link commits that have no meaningful session content to restore
- Deletions have no content - just the absence of a file
- Both hooks should agree (my fix makes them consistent)

**One nuance:** If the agent deleted a file via Bash `rm`, it's not tracked in `FilesTouched` anyway since Bash commands don't have `file_path` parameters. The only way a deletion ends up in carry-forward is if:
1. Agent modified the file earlier (tracked via Write)
2. Then deleted it via Bash (not tracked)
3. User commits the modification first, then the deletion

In that case, the modification commit has the meaningful checkpoint. The deletion commit adding another checkpoint would be redundant.

So yes, this fix makes sense - skipping deletions in both hooks is the correct behavior.

> DEVELOPER

no, this is not the theme of the PR, we do allow duplicated session logs, the context should always be there when looking at a single commit

> AGENT

You're right, I misunderstood. The goal is that when looking at any commit, you should be able to see the session context that led to it. Duplicate session logs are fine - they're intentional.

So for the deletion commit:
- If the deletion is part of the agent's work, it SHOULD get a checkpoint trailer
- The session SHOULD be condensed so the context is available

The correct fix is the opposite - make `filesOverlapWithContent` (post-commit) recognize deletions as overlap, not make `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent` skip them.

Let me revert my change and fix `filesOverlapWithContent` instead:

```
   122→			return true
   123→		}
   124→
   125→		// For new files, check content against shadow branch
   126→		shadowFile, err := shadowTree.File(filePath)
   127→		if err != nil {
   128→			// File not in shadow branch - this shouldn't happen but skip it
   129→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: file in filesTouched but not in shadow branch",
   130→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   131→			)
   132→			continue
   133→		}
   134→
   135→		// Compare by hash (blob hash) - exact content match required for new files
   136→		if headFile.Hash == shadowFile.Hash {
   137→			logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content match found",
   138→				slog.String("file", filePath),
   139→				slog.String("hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   140→			)
   141→			return true
   142→		}
   143→
   144→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: new file content mismatch (may be reverted & replaced)",
   145→			slog.String("file", filePath),
   146→			slog.String("head_hash", headFile.Hash.String()),
   147→			slog.String("shadow_hash", shadowFile.Hash.String()),
   148→		)
   149→	}
   150→
   151→	logging.Debug(logCtx, "filesOverlapWithContent: no overlapping files found",
   152→		slog.Int("files_checked", len(filesTouched)),
   153→	)
   154→	return false
   155→}
```

```json
  "old_string": "\t// Check each file in filesTouched\n\tfor _, filePath := range filesTouched {\n\t\t// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)\n\t\theadFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t// File not in HEAD commit. This happens when:\n\t\t\t// - The session created/modified the file but user deleted it before committing\n\t\t\t// - The file was staged as a deletion (git rm)\n\t\t\t// In both cases, the session's work on this file is not in the commit,\n\t\t\t// so it doesn't contribute to overlap. Continue checking other files.\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file\n\t\tisModified := false\n\t\tif parentTree != nil {\n\t\t\tif _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {\n\t\t\t\tisModified = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)\n\t\tif isModified {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", filePath),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}",
  "new_string": "\t// Check each file in filesTouched\n\tfor _, filePath := range filesTouched {\n\t\t// Get file from HEAD tree (the committed content)\n\t\theadFile, err := headTree.File(filePath)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t// File not in HEAD commit. Check if this is a deletion (existed in parent).\n\t\t\t// Deletions count as overlap because the agent's action (deleting the file)\n\t\t\t// is being committed - we want the session context linked to this commit.\n\t\t\tif parentTree != nil {\n\t\t\t\tif _, parentErr := parentTree.File(filePath); parentErr == nil {\n\t\t\t\t\t// File existed in parent but not in HEAD - this is a deletion\n\t\t\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"filesOverlapWithContent: deleted file counts as overlap\",\n\t\t\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", filePath),\n\t\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// File didn't exist in parent either - not a deletion, skip\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Check if this is a modified file (exists in parent) or new file\n\t\tisModified := false\n\t\tif parentTree != nil {\n\t\t\tif _, err := parentTree.File(filePath); err == nil {\n\t\t\t\tisModified = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Modified files always count as overlap (user edited session's work)\n\t\tif isModified {\n\t\t\tlogging.Debug(logCtx, \"filesOverlapWithContent: modified file counts as overlap\",\n\t\t\t\tslog.String(\"file\", filePath),\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\treturn true\n\t\t}"
}
```

```json
  "new_string": "// TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_DeletedFile tests that a deleted file\n// (exists in HEAD but staged for deletion) DOES count as overlap.\n// The agent's action of deleting the file is being committed, so the session\n// context should be linked to this commit.\nfunc TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_DeletedFile(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\n\tdir := t.TempDir()\n\trepo, err := git.PlainInit(dir, false)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\tworktree, err := repo.Worktree()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\t// Create and commit a file that will be deleted\n\tfilePath := filepath.Join(dir, \"to_delete.txt\")\n\terr = os.WriteFile(filePath, []byte(\"original content\"), 0644)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\t_, err = worktree.Add(\"to_delete.txt\")\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\t_, err = worktree.Commit(\"Add to_delete.txt\", &git.CommitOptions{\n\t\tAuthor: &object.Signature{\n\t\t\tName:  \"Test\",\n\t\t\tEmail: \"test@test.com\",\n\t\t\tWhen:  time.Now(),\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\t// Create shadow branch (simulating agent work on the file)\n\tcreateShadowBranchWithContent(t, repo, \"mno7890\", \"e3b0c4\", map[string][]byte{\n\t\t\"to_delete.txt\": []byte(\"agent modified content\"),\n\t})\n\n\t// Stage the file for deletion (git rm)\n\t_, err = worktree.Remove(\"to_delete.txt\")\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\t// Get shadow tree\n\tshadowBranch := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(\"mno7890\", \"e3b0c4\")\n\tshadowRef, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranch), true)\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tshadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(shadowRef.Hash())\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\tshadowTree, err := shadowCommit.Tree()\n\trequire.NoError(t, err)\n\n\t// Deleted file SHOULD count as overlap - the agent's deletion is being committed\n\tresult := stagedFilesOverlapWithContent(repo, shadowTree, []string{\"to_delete.txt\"}, []string{\"to_delete.txt\"})\n\tassert.True(t, result, \"Deleted file should count as overlap (agent's deletion being committed)\")\n}"
```

> AGENT

All tests pass. Here's the corrected fix:

## Summary

**The correct behavior**: When looking at any commit, you should be able to see the session context. Deletions that are part of the agent's work should get checkpoint trailers and be condensed.

**The bug**: `filesOverlapWithContent` (post-commit) was skipping deleted files, while `stagedFilesOverlapWithContent` (prepare-commit-msg) correctly recognized them as overlap. This caused orphaned checkpoint IDs.

**The fix**: Made `filesOverlapWithContent` recognize deletions:
```go
if err != nil {
    // File not in HEAD commit. Check if this is a deletion (existed in parent).
    // Deletions count as overlap because the agent's action (deleting the file)
    // is being committed - we want the session context linked to this commit.
    if parentTree != nil {
        if _, parentErr := parentTree.File(filePath); parentErr == nil {
            // File existed in parent but not in HEAD - this is a deletion
            return true
        }
    }
    continue
}
```

**Tests added**:
- `TestFilesOverlapWithContent_DeletedFile` - verifies deletions count as overlap in post-commit
- `TestStagedFilesOverlapWithContent_DeletedFile` - verifies deletions count as overlap in prepare-commit-msg

Now both hooks agree: deletions of files in `filesTouched` count as overlap, so the checkpoint trailer is added AND the session is condensed.
